# The Integers — Primes

A literate `bpa` transliteration of **Chapter 2 ("The Integers"), §2.2 (Greatest
Common Divisors and Primes)** of Thomas W. Judson's *Abstract Algebra* (source:
`vendor/aata/src/integers.xml`; © 1997–2026 Judson, GFDL 1.3). Run
`bpa check aata/2.3-primes.md`.

This section proves the **GCD / Bézout theorem** and its **coprime corollary**,
following Judson's argument. They build on the Division Algorithm proved in
`std/integer-divides.bpa` (§2.2). We bring in that library, the ℤ order,
divisibility, and the Well-Ordering schema, naming the arithmetic vocabulary
this section will use. Because there is no `gcd` function, we *characterize* the
greatest common divisor by its defining properties (positive, a common divisor,
divisible by every common divisor).

```bpa
import int_divides <<< "std/integer-divides.bpa"
import order <<< "std/integer-order.bpa"
import ring <<< "std/integer.bpa"
import wellordering <<< "std/integer-wellordering.bpa"

sort Int = int_divides.Int
const ZERO = int_divides.ZERO
const ONE = int_divides.ONE
func succ = int_divides.succ
func prev = order.prev
func add = int_divides.add
func mul = int_divides.mul
func sub = int_divides.sub
func neg = int_divides.neg
pred less_than = int_divides.less_than
pred at_most = int_divides.at_most
pred is_nonneg = int_divides.is_nonneg
pred does_divide = int_divides.divides

theorem divisionAlgorithmExists = int_divides.divisionAlgorithmExists
theorem divisionAlgorithmUnique = int_divides.divisionAlgorithmUnique
axiom oneIsSuccZero = int_divides.oneIsSuccZero

// -- order and nonneg vocabulary the argument names explicitly --------------
theorem trichotomy = order.trichotomy
theorem lessThanIrreflexive = order.lessThanIrreflexive
theorem lessThanTransitive = order.lessThanTransitive
theorem lessThanToLessOrEqual = order.lessThanToLessOrEqual
theorem lessOrEqualSplit = order.lessOrEqualSplit
theorem lessThanSuccRight = order.lessThanSuccRight
theorem negLessThanZero = order.negLessThanZero
theorem nonnegCases = order.nonnegCases
axiom lessThanElim = order.lessThanElim
axiom lessThanIntro = order.lessThanIntro
axiom nonnegZero = order.nonnegZero
axiom nonnegSucc = order.nonnegSucc
axiom addZeroLeft = order.addZeroLeft
axiom addSuccLeft = order.addSuccLeft
axiom prevSucc = int_divides.prevSucc

// -- divisibility intro/elim and the closure lemmas -------------------------
// `does_divide` is now a transparent `define` (= `int_divides.divides(d, n)` = the
// existential `exists k; n = mul(d, k)`), so the old `dividesIntro`/`dividesElim`
// AXIOMS are gone. Recover them here as local THEOREMS proved from the definition:
// intro is `exists_intro`, elim is the identity (the pred IS the existential).
theorem dividesMul = int_divides.dividesMul
theorem dividesAdd = int_divides.dividesAdd

theorem dividesIntro: forall n, d, k: Int; n = mul(d, k) -> does_divide(d, n)
proof
  @generalize-n |
    fix n: Int {
      @generalize-d |
        fix d: Int {
          @generalize-k |
            fix k: Int {
              @given-n-is-dk |
                assume n = mul(d, k) {
                  @n-is-dk |
                    n = mul(d, k)
                    [by hypothesis given-n-is-dk]
                  @some-k-works |
                    does_divide(d, n)
                    [by exists_intro(k) n-is-dk]
                }
              @intro-at-n-d-k |
                n = mul(d, k) -> does_divide(d, n)
                [by implies_intro given-n-is-dk]
            }
          @discharge-k |
            forall k: Int; n = mul(d, k) -> does_divide(d, n)
            [by forall_intro generalize-k]
        }
      @discharge-d |
        forall d, k: Int; n = mul(d, k) -> does_divide(d, n)
        [by forall_intro generalize-d]
    }
  @conclusion |
    forall n, d, k: Int; n = mul(d, k) -> does_divide(d, n)
    [by forall_intro generalize-n]
qed

theorem dividesElim: forall d, m: Int; does_divide(d, m) -> exists k: Int; m = mul(d, k)
proof
  @generalize-d |
    fix d: Int {
      @generalize-m |
        fix m: Int {
          @given-d-divides-m |
            assume does_divide(d, m) {
              @the-existential |
                exists k: Int; m = mul(d, k)
                [by hypothesis given-d-divides-m]
            }
          @elim-at-d-m |
            does_divide(d, m) -> exists k: Int; m = mul(d, k)
            [by implies_intro given-d-divides-m]
        }
      @discharge-m |
        forall m: Int; does_divide(d, m) -> exists k: Int; m = mul(d, k)
        [by forall_intro generalize-m]
    }
  @conclusion |
    forall d, m: Int; does_divide(d, m) -> exists k: Int; m = mul(d, k)
    [by forall_intro generalize-d]
qed

// -- ring rearrangements the book elides ------------------------------------
axiom definitionOfSubtraction = order.definitionOfSubtraction
theorem mulOneRight = ring.mulOneRight
theorem mulZeroRight = ring.mulZeroRight
theorem mulNegRight = ring.mulNegRight
theorem mulAddDistribLeft = ring.mulAddDistribLeft
theorem mulAddDistribRight = ring.mulAddDistribRight
theorem mulIsAssociative = ring.mulIsAssociative
theorem addIsCommutative = ring.addIsCommutative
theorem addIsAssociative = ring.addIsAssociative
theorem addZeroRight = ring.addZeroRight
theorem addSuccRight = ring.addSuccRight
theorem addNegRight = ring.addNegRight
theorem addNegLeft = ring.addNegLeft
theorem negNeg = ring.negNeg
theorem negAdd = ring.negAdd
theorem addCancelLeft = order.addCancelLeft

// -- the Well-Ordering schema ------------------------------------------------
theorem nonnegWellOrdering = wellordering.nonnegWellOrdering
```

A positive integer is in particular nonnegative — the fact that lets the
Well-Ordering schema's guard (`is_nonneg`) be discharged from `less_than(ZERO, x)`.
Unfolding `less_than(ZERO, x)` gives a nonnegative gap `d` with `ZERO + succ(d) = x`;
since `add(ZERO, succ(d)) = succ(d)` and the successor of a nonnegative is
nonnegative, `x` itself is nonnegative.

```bpa
theorem positiveIsNonneg = order.positiveIsNonneg
```

The Bézout argument will, at its crux, learn of a remainder `r'` that is both
nonnegative and *not* positive, and must conclude `r' = ZERO`. A nonnegative
integer splits (`nonnegCases`) into `ZERO` or a successor `succ(m)` of a
nonnegative `m`; a successor is positive (`add(ZERO, succ(m)) = succ(m)`, so
`lessThanIntro` gives `less_than(ZERO, succ(m))`), so if the integer is *not*
positive the successor case is impossible and it must be `ZERO`.

```bpa
theorem nonnegNotPositiveIsZero: forall x: Int; is_nonneg(x) -> (not less_than(ZERO, x)) -> x = ZERO
proof
  @generalize-x |
    fix x: Int {
      @given-x-nonneg |
        assume is_nonneg(x) {
          @given-x-not-positive |
            assume not less_than(ZERO, x) {
              @x-nonneg |
                is_nonneg(x)
                [by hypothesis given-x-nonneg]
              @x-not-positive |
                not less_than(ZERO, x)
                [by hypothesis given-x-not-positive]
              @x-zero-or-successor |
                x = ZERO or (exists m: Int; is_nonneg(m) and x = succ(m))
                [using specialize nonnegCases(x) x-nonneg]
              @conclusion-x-is-zero |
                x = ZERO
                case x-zero-or-successor {
                  @when-x-is-zero |
                    assume x = ZERO {
                      @conclusion-x-is-zero |
                        x = ZERO
                        [by hypothesis when-x-is-zero]
                    }
                  @when-x-is-successor |
                    assume exists m: Int; is_nonneg(m) and x = succ(m) {
                      @successor-exists |
                        exists m: Int; is_nonneg(m) and x = succ(m)
                        [by hypothesis when-x-is-successor]
                      @with-predecessor-m |
                        unpack m: Int from successor-exists {
                          @gap-nonneg-and-x-is-successor |
                            is_nonneg(m) and x = succ(m)
                            [by hypothesis with-predecessor-m]
                          @gap-nonneg |
                            is_nonneg(m)
                            [by and_elim_left gap-nonneg-and-x-is-successor]
                          @x-is-successor |
                            x = succ(m)
                            [by and_elim_right gap-nonneg-and-x-is-successor]
                          @successor-is-x |
                            succ(m) = x
                            [using arithmetic x-is-successor]
                          @zero-plus-successor-is-x |
                            add(ZERO, succ(m)) = x
                            [using arithmetic successor-is-x]
                          @x-is-positive |
                            less_than(ZERO, x)
                            [using specialize lessThanIntro(ZERO, m, x) gap-nonneg zero-plus-successor-is-x]
                          @conclusion-x-is-zero |
                            x = ZERO
                            [by absurd x-is-positive x-not-positive]
                        }
                      @conclusion-x-is-zero |
                        x = ZERO
                        [by exists_elim with-predecessor-m]
                    }
                }
            }
          @not-positive-implies-zero |
            (not less_than(ZERO, x)) -> x = ZERO
            [by implies_intro given-x-not-positive]
        }
      @nonneg-implies-not-positive-implies-zero |
        is_nonneg(x) -> (not less_than(ZERO, x)) -> x = ZERO
        [by implies_intro given-x-nonneg]
    }
  @conclusion |
    forall x: Int; is_nonneg(x) -> (not less_than(ZERO, x)) -> x = ZERO
    [by forall_intro generalize-x]
qed
```

Judson's nonemptiness argument splits on the sign of `a`: when `a` is positive
the witness `a·1 + b·0 = a` is a positive member; when `a` is negative the
witness `a·(−1) + b·0 = −a` is, and this needs that the negation of a negative
integer is positive. Unfolding `less_than(a, ZERO)` gives a nonnegative gap `d` with
`add(a, succ(d)) = ZERO`; adding `−a` on the left and cancelling `a` shows
`succ(d) = −a`, and `lessThanIntro` reads `less_than(ZERO, −a)` off that.

```bpa
theorem negativeNegIsPositive = order.negativeNegIsPositive
```

The next proof (`remainderIsMember`) leans on the `arithmetic` certifier to
discharge an elided linear cancellation, and that certifier resolves its rewrite
rules by name from what is already in scope. So we pull `addLeftSwap` (the
additive left-swap — proved identically to `mulLeftSwap` below) and the
numeral-lifting sign rules up to here. **TEMPORARY placement:** `addLeftSwap`
belongs later in Judson's build order (with `mulLeftSwap`); the model-spaces
refactor, which lets the certifier find its rule lemmas without pre-declaration,
will move it back.

```bpa
theorem addLeftSwap: forall a, b, c: Int; add(a, add(b, c)) = add(b, add(a, c))
proof
  @generalize-a |
    fix a: Int {
      @generalize-b |
        fix b: Int {
          @generalize-c |
            fix c: Int {
              @abc-regroups-flat |
                add(add(a, b), c) = add(a, add(b, c))
                [using specialize addIsAssociative(a, b, c)]
              @a-b-swaps |
                add(a, b) = add(b, a)
                [using specialize addIsCommutative(a, b)]
              @ba-c-regroups |
                add(add(b, a), c) = add(b, add(a, c))
                [using specialize addIsAssociative(b, a, c)]
              @conclusion-add-left-swap |
                add(a, add(b, c)) = add(b, add(a, c))
                [using chain abc-regroups-flat a-b-swaps ba-c-regroups]
            }
          @discharge-c |
            forall c: Int; add(a, add(b, c)) = add(b, add(a, c))
            [by forall_intro generalize-c]
        }
      @discharge-b |
        forall b, c: Int; add(a, add(b, c)) = add(b, add(a, c))
        [by forall_intro generalize-b]
    }
  @conclusion |
    forall a, b, c: Int; add(a, add(b, c)) = add(b, add(a, c))
    [by forall_intro generalize-a]
qed

// numeral-lifting rules for the `arithmetic` certifier: a neg(succ(ZERO))
// summand reduces (negSucc/negZero) to a prev-tower that addPrevLeft/Right float
// up to the tower prefix, so cancellations like add(ONE, sub(p, ONE)) = p certify.
axiom negSucc = int_divides.negSucc
axiom addPrevLeft = int_divides.addPrevLeft
theorem addPrevRight = int_divides.addPrevRight
axiom succPrev = int_divides.succPrev
```

The heart of Judson's argument that `d = ar + bs` divides `a` runs the Division
Algorithm to write `a = dq + r'` and shows the remainder `r'` is *itself* a
member of `S`: substituting `d = ar + bs`, Judson computes `r' = a − dq =
a − (ar + bs)q = a(1 − rq) + b(−sq)` in one line. We isolate that elided ring
identity here. From `a = dq + r'` we recover `r' = a − dq` by cancelling `dq`
(the linear cancellation `dq + (a − dq) = a` that `arithmetic` discharges, then
`addCancelLeft`); substituting `d` and rearranging by distributing and collecting
the `a` and `b` coefficients (`simplify` over the ring lemmas) gives the member
form `r' = a(1 − rq) + b(−sq)`.

```bpa
theorem remainderIsMember:
  forall a, b, d, r, s, q, remainder: Int;
  a = add(mul(d, q), remainder) -> d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s))
  -> remainder = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
proof
  @generalize-a |
    fix a: Int {
      @generalize-b |
        fix b: Int {
          @generalize-d |
            fix d: Int {
              @generalize-r |
                fix r: Int {
                  @generalize-s |
                    fix s: Int {
                      @generalize-q |
                        fix q: Int {
                          @generalize-remainder |
                            fix remainder: Int {
                              @given-a-decomposes |
                                assume a = add(mul(d, q), remainder) {
                                  @given-d-value |
                                    assume d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) {
                                      @a-decomposes |
                                        a = add(mul(d, q), remainder)
                                        [by hypothesis given-a-decomposes]
                                      @d-value |
                                        d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s))
                                        [by hypothesis given-d-value]
                                      // remainder = a - dq: cancel dq off a = dq + remainder.
                                      // dq + (a - dq) = a is the elided linear cancellation.
                                      @dq-plus-difference-is-a |
                                        add(mul(d, q), sub(a, mul(d, q))) = a
                                        [using arithmetic]
                                      @dq-plus-difference-is-decomposition |
                                        add(mul(d, q), sub(a, mul(d, q))) = add(mul(d, q), remainder)
                                        [by rewrite a-decomposes dq-plus-difference-is-a]
                                      @cancel-left |
                                        forall c, x, y: Int; add(c, x) = add(c, y) -> x = y
                                        [by cite addCancelLeft]
                                      @cancel-the-quotient-term |
                                        add(mul(d, q), sub(a, mul(d, q))) = add(mul(d, q), remainder) -> sub(a, mul(d, q)) = remainder
                                        [by forall_elim(mul(d, q), sub(a, mul(d, q)), remainder) cancel-left]
                                      @difference-is-remainder |
                                        sub(a, mul(d, q)) = remainder
                                        [by modus_ponens cancel-the-quotient-term dq-plus-difference-is-decomposition]
                                      // substitute d = ar + bs into a - dq, then the elided algebra
                                      // a - (ar + bs)q = a(1 - rq) + b(-sq).
                                      @difference-collects-coefficients |
                                        sub(a, mul(add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)), q)) = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
                                        [using simplify definitionOfSubtraction mulAddDistribLeft mulAddDistribRight mulNegRight mulOneRight mulIsAssociative addIsAssociative negAdd]
                                      @conclusion-remainder-is-member |
                                        remainder = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
                                        [using chain difference-is-remainder d-value difference-collects-coefficients]
                                    }
                                  @d-value-implies-member |
                                    d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) -> remainder = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
                                    [by implies_intro given-d-value]
                                }
                              @conclusion-implication |
                                a = add(mul(d, q), remainder) -> d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) -> remainder = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
                                [by implies_intro given-a-decomposes]
                            }
                          @discharge-remainder |
                            forall remainder: Int; a = add(mul(d, q), remainder) -> d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) -> remainder = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
                            [by forall_intro generalize-remainder]
                        }
                      @discharge-q |
                        forall q, remainder: Int; a = add(mul(d, q), remainder) -> d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) -> remainder = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
                        [by forall_intro generalize-q]
                    }
                  @discharge-s |
                    forall s, q, remainder: Int; a = add(mul(d, q), remainder) -> d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) -> remainder = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
                    [by forall_intro generalize-s]
                }
              @discharge-r |
                forall r, s, q, remainder: Int; a = add(mul(d, q), remainder) -> d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) -> remainder = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
                [by forall_intro generalize-r]
            }
          @discharge-d |
            forall d, r, s, q, remainder: Int; a = add(mul(d, q), remainder) -> d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) -> remainder = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
            [by forall_intro generalize-d]
        }
      @discharge-b |
        forall b, d, r, s, q, remainder: Int; a = add(mul(d, q), remainder) -> d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) -> remainder = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
        [by forall_intro generalize-b]
    }
  @conclusion |
    forall a, b, d, r, s, q, remainder: Int;
      a = add(mul(d, q), remainder) -> d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s))
      -> remainder = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
    [by forall_intro generalize-a]
qed
```

The coprime corollary will produce a positive common divisor `d` that must equal
`ONE` or `−ONE`; the second alternative is ruled out because `−1` is *less_than*
zero, so it cannot also be positive. We record that `−1 < 0` here: `−1` is
nonnegatively-gapped less_than `0` by `succ(ZERO) = ONE`, since
`add(neg(ONE), succ(ZERO)) = add(neg(ONE), ONE) = ZERO`, and `lessThanIntro`
reads `less_than(neg(ONE), ZERO)` off that.

```bpa
theorem negOneLessThanZero = order.negOneLessThanZero
```

Before invoking Well-Ordering, Judson must exhibit *some* positive member of
`S = {am + bn : am + bn > 0}` — the set is nonempty because `|a| ∈ S`. Since
`a ≠ 0`, trichotomy splits `a` into positive or negative (the `a = 0` case is
excluded). If `a > 0`, the combination `a·1 + b·0 = a` is a positive member; if
`a < 0`, then `−a > 0` (`negativeNegIsPositive`) and `a·(−1) + b·0 = −a` is a
positive member. Either way the witness is positive, hence nonnegative
(`positiveIsNonneg`), discharging the schema's guard. We package this nonemptiness
as a schema in `a`, `b`, so its conclusion matches the exact shape the
Well-Ordering instance less_than consumes.

```bpa
theorem combinationSetNonempty(a: Int, b: Int):
  a != ZERO
  -> exists n: Int; is_nonneg(n) and (less_than(ZERO, n) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; n = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))))
proof
  @given-a-nonzero |
    assume a != ZERO {
      @a-nonzero |
        a != ZERO
        [by hypothesis given-a-nonzero]
      @a-sign-split |
        less_than(a, ZERO) or a = ZERO or less_than(ZERO, a)
        [using specialize trichotomy(a, ZERO)]
      @conclusion-set-nonempty |
        exists n: Int; is_nonneg(n) and (less_than(ZERO, n) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; n = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))))
        case a-sign-split {
          @when-a-negative |
            assume less_than(a, ZERO) {
              @a-negative |
                less_than(a, ZERO)
                [by hypothesis when-a-negative]
              @neg-a-positive |
                less_than(ZERO, neg(a))
                [using specialize negativeNegIsPositive(a) a-negative]
              @positive-is-nonneg |
                forall x: Int; less_than(ZERO, x) -> is_nonneg(x)
                [by cite positiveIsNonneg]
              @positive-is-nonneg-at-neg-a |
                less_than(ZERO, neg(a)) -> is_nonneg(neg(a))
                [by forall_elim(neg(a)) positive-is-nonneg]
              @neg-a-nonneg |
                is_nonneg(neg(a))
                [by modus_ponens positive-is-nonneg-at-neg-a neg-a-positive]
              @neg-a-is-combination |
                neg(a) = add(mul(a, neg(ONE)), mul(b, ZERO))
                [using simplify mulNegRight mulOneRight mulZeroRight addZeroRight]
              @neg-a-is-some-combination-in-nn |
                exists nn: Int; neg(a) = add(mul(a, neg(ONE)), mul(b, nn))
                [by exists_intro(ZERO) neg-a-is-combination]
              @neg-a-is-a-combination |
                exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; neg(a) = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))
                [by exists_intro(neg(ONE)) neg-a-is-some-combination-in-nn]
              @neg-a-positive-and-combination |
                less_than(ZERO, neg(a)) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; neg(a) = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))
                [by and_intro neg-a-positive neg-a-is-a-combination]
              @neg-a-is-nonneg-member |
                is_nonneg(neg(a)) and (less_than(ZERO, neg(a)) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; neg(a) = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))))
                [by and_intro neg-a-nonneg neg-a-positive-and-combination]
              @conclusion-set-nonempty |
                exists n: Int; is_nonneg(n) and (less_than(ZERO, n) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; n = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))))
                [by exists_intro(neg(a)) neg-a-is-nonneg-member]
            }
          @when-a-zero |
            assume a = ZERO {
              @a-is-zero |
                a = ZERO
                [by hypothesis when-a-zero]
              @conclusion-set-nonempty |
                exists n: Int; is_nonneg(n) and (less_than(ZERO, n) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; n = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))))
                [by absurd a-is-zero a-nonzero]
            }
          @when-a-positive |
            assume less_than(ZERO, a) {
              @a-positive |
                less_than(ZERO, a)
                [by hypothesis when-a-positive]
              @a-nonneg |
                is_nonneg(a)
                [using specialize positiveIsNonneg(a) a-positive]
              @a-is-combination |
                a = add(mul(a, ONE), mul(b, ZERO))
                [using simplify mulOneRight mulZeroRight addZeroRight]
              @a-is-some-combination-in-nn |
                exists nn: Int; a = add(mul(a, ONE), mul(b, nn))
                [by exists_intro(ZERO) a-is-combination]
              @a-is-a-combination |
                exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; a = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))
                [by exists_intro(ONE) a-is-some-combination-in-nn]
              @a-positive-and-combination |
                less_than(ZERO, a) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; a = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))
                [by and_intro a-positive a-is-a-combination]
              @a-is-nonneg-member |
                is_nonneg(a) and (less_than(ZERO, a) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; a = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))))
                [by and_intro a-nonneg a-positive-and-combination]
              @conclusion-set-nonempty |
                exists n: Int; is_nonneg(n) and (less_than(ZERO, n) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; n = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))))
                [by exists_intro(a) a-is-nonneg-member]
            }
        }
    }
  @conclusion |
    a != ZERO
      -> exists n: Int; is_nonneg(n) and (less_than(ZERO, n) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; n = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))))
    [by implies_intro given-a-nonzero]
qed
```

## The GCD / Bézout Theorem

> **Theorem 2.10.** Let $a$ and $b$ be nonzero integers. Then there exist integers
> $r$ and $s$ such that $\gcd(a,b) = ar + bs$. Furthermore, the greatest common
> divisor of $a$ and $b$ is unique.

There is no `gcd` *function* in this development, so we prove the theorem in the
form that *characterizes* the gcd by its defining properties. `bezout` asserts a
single positive integer `d` that (i) divides `a` and `b`, (ii) is divisible by
every common divisor `e`, and (iii) is an integer combination `d = ar + bs`.
Uniqueness is then automatic: any two integers with property (ii) divide each
other, and — being positive — are equal; and (iii) is Bézout's identity itself.

Judson's proof: let `S = {am + bn : am + bn > 0}`. `S` is nonempty
(`combinationSetNonempty`), and every member is positive hence nonnegative, so
Well-Ordering hands us a *least* member `d = ar + bs`. We claim `d = gcd(a,b)`.
Running the Division Algorithm on `a` by `d` gives `a = dq + r'` with
`0 ≤ r' < d`. Were `r' > 0`, the identity `r' = a(1 − rq) + b(−sq)`
(`remainderIsMember`) would make `r'` a *member of `S` smaller than `d`*,
contradicting minimality; so `r' = 0` and `d ∣ a`. Symmetrically `d ∣ b`. Finally
any common divisor `e` of `a`, `b` divides `ar + bs = d`, so `d` is the greatest.

```bpa
theorem bezout: forall a, b: Int; a != ZERO -> b != ZERO ->
  exists d: Int;
  less_than(ZERO, d)
  and does_divide(d, a) and does_divide(d, b)
  and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d))
  and (exists r: Int; exists s: Int; d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)))
proof
  @generalize-a |
    fix a: Int {
      @generalize-b |
        fix b: Int {
          @given-a-nonzero |
            assume a != ZERO {
              @given-b-nonzero |
                assume b != ZERO {
                  @a-nonzero |
                    a != ZERO
                    [by hypothesis given-a-nonzero]
                  // Nonemptiness of S = {am + bn > 0}, from combinationSetNonempty.
                  @set-nonempty-if-a-nonzero |
                    a != ZERO
                      -> exists n: Int; is_nonneg(n) and (less_than(ZERO, n) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; n = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))))
                    [using instantiation combinationSetNonempty(a, b)]
                  @set-is-nonempty |
                    exists n: Int; is_nonneg(n) and (less_than(ZERO, n) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; n = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))))
                    [by modus_ponens set-nonempty-if-a-nonzero a-nonzero]
                  // Well-Ordering: S has a least member d.
                  @well-ordering-on-S |
                    (exists n: Int; is_nonneg(n) and (less_than(ZERO, n) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; n = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))))
                      -> exists least: Int; is_nonneg(least) and (less_than(ZERO, least) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; least = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))) and (forall m: Int; is_nonneg(m) -> (less_than(ZERO, m) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; m = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))) -> at_most(least, m))
                    [using instantiation nonnegWellOrdering((fun x: Int => less_than(ZERO, x) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; x = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))))]
                  @least-member-exists |
                    exists least: Int; is_nonneg(least) and (less_than(ZERO, least) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; least = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))) and (forall m: Int; is_nonneg(m) -> (less_than(ZERO, m) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; m = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))) -> at_most(least, m))
                    [by modus_ponens well-ordering-on-S set-is-nonempty]
                  @with-least-member-d |
                    unpack d: Int from least-member-exists {
                      @d-is-least-member |
                        is_nonneg(d) and (less_than(ZERO, d) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; d = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))) and (forall m: Int; is_nonneg(m) -> (less_than(ZERO, m) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; m = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))) -> at_most(d, m))
                        [by hypothesis with-least-member-d]
                      @d-is-positive |
                        less_than(ZERO, d)
                        [using tautology d-is-least-member]
                      @d-is-a-combination |
                        exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; d = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))
                        [using tautology d-is-least-member]
                      @d-is-least |
                        forall m: Int; is_nonneg(m) -> (less_than(ZERO, m) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; m = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))) -> at_most(d, m)
                        [using tautology d-is-least-member]
                      // name the coefficients: d = ar + bs.
                      @with-coefficient-r |
                        unpack r: Int from d-is-a-combination {
                          @d-is-combination-in-r |
                            exists nn: Int; d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, nn))
                            [by hypothesis with-coefficient-r]
                          @with-coefficient-s |
                            unpack s: Int from d-is-combination-in-r {
                              @d-equals-combination |
                                d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s))
                                [by hypothesis with-coefficient-s]
                              // d | a: run the Division Algorithm a = dq + r'.
                              @a-divided-by-d |
                                exists q: Int; exists rem: Int; a = add(mul(d, q), rem) and is_nonneg(rem) and less_than(rem, d)
                                [using specialize divisionAlgorithmExists(a, d) d-is-positive]
                              @with-quotient-q |
                                unpack q: Int from a-divided-by-d {
                                  @a-remainder-exists |
                                    exists rem: Int; a = add(mul(d, q), rem) and is_nonneg(rem) and less_than(rem, d)
                                    [by hypothesis with-quotient-q]
                                  @with-remainder |
                                    unpack rem: Int from a-remainder-exists {
                                      @a-decomposes-and-remainder-bounded |
                                        a = add(mul(d, q), rem) and is_nonneg(rem) and less_than(rem, d)
                                        [by hypothesis with-remainder]
                                      @a-decomposes |
                                        a = add(mul(d, q), rem)
                                        [using tautology a-decomposes-and-remainder-bounded]
                                      @remainder-nonneg |
                                        is_nonneg(rem)
                                        [using tautology a-decomposes-and-remainder-bounded]
                                      @remainder-less_than-d |
                                        less_than(rem, d)
                                        [using tautology a-decomposes-and-remainder-bounded]
                                      // a positive remainder would be a member of S smaller than d.
                                      @d-not-less_than-itself |
                                        not less_than(d, d)
                                        [using specialize lessThanIrreflexive(d)]
                                      @suppose-remainder-positive |
                                        assume less_than(ZERO, rem) {
                                          @remainder-positive |
                                            less_than(ZERO, rem)
                                            [by hypothesis suppose-remainder-positive]
                                          @remainder-is-member-identity |
                                            a = add(mul(d, q), rem) -> d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) -> rem = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
                                            [using specialize remainderIsMember(a, b, d, r, s, q, rem)]
                                          @remainder-is-member-form |
                                            rem = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, neg(mul(s, q))))
                                            [using tautology remainder-is-member-identity a-decomposes d-equals-combination]
                                          @remainder-is-some-combination-in-nn |
                                            exists nn: Int; rem = add(mul(a, sub(ONE, mul(r, q))), mul(b, nn))
                                            [by exists_intro(neg(mul(s, q))) remainder-is-member-form]
                                          @remainder-is-a-combination |
                                            exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; rem = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))
                                            [by exists_intro(sub(ONE, mul(r, q))) remainder-is-some-combination-in-nn]
                                          @remainder-is-positive-combination |
                                            less_than(ZERO, rem) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; rem = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))
                                            [by and_intro remainder-positive remainder-is-a-combination]
                                          @remainder-least-comparison |
                                            is_nonneg(rem) -> (less_than(ZERO, rem) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; rem = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))) -> at_most(d, rem)
                                            [by forall_elim(rem) d-is-least]
                                          @d-at-most-remainder |
                                            at_most(d, rem)
                                            [using tautology remainder-least-comparison remainder-nonneg remainder-is-positive-combination]
                                          @less-or-equal-split-at-d-remainder |
                                            at_most(d, rem) -> (less_than(d, rem) or d = rem)
                                            [using specialize lessOrEqualSplit(d, rem)]
                                          @d-less_than-or-equal-remainder |
                                            less_than(d, rem) or d = rem
                                            [by modus_ponens less-or-equal-split-at-d-remainder d-at-most-remainder]
                                          @remainder-would-be-a-smaller-member |
                                            less_than(d, d)
                                            case d-less_than-or-equal-remainder {
                                              @when-d-less_than-remainder |
                                                assume less_than(d, rem) {
                                                  @d-less_than-remainder |
                                                    less_than(d, rem)
                                                    [by hypothesis when-d-less_than-remainder]
                                                  @transitive-at-d-remainder-d |
                                                    less_than(d, rem) -> less_than(rem, d) -> less_than(d, d)
                                                    [using specialize lessThanTransitive(d, rem, d)]
                                                  @conclusion-d-less_than-itself |
                                                    less_than(d, d)
                                                    [using tautology transitive-at-d-remainder-d d-less_than-remainder remainder-less_than-d]
                                                }
                                              @when-d-equals-remainder |
                                                assume d = rem {
                                                  @d-equals-remainder |
                                                    d = rem
                                                    [by hypothesis when-d-equals-remainder]
                                                  @remainder-equals-d |
                                                    rem = d
                                                    [using arithmetic d-equals-remainder]
                                                  @conclusion-d-less_than-itself |
                                                    less_than(d, d)
                                                    [by rewrite remainder-equals-d remainder-less_than-d]
                                                }
                                            }
                                        }
                                      @remainder-not-positive |
                                        not less_than(ZERO, rem)
                                        [by not_intro suppose-remainder-positive remainder-would-be-a-smaller-member d-not-less_than-itself]
                                      // nonneg and not positive forces the remainder to be zero.
                                      @nonneg-not-positive-is-zero |
                                        is_nonneg(rem) -> (not less_than(ZERO, rem)) -> rem = ZERO
                                        [using specialize nonnegNotPositiveIsZero(rem)]
                                      @remainder-is-zero |
                                        rem = ZERO
                                        [using tautology nonneg-not-positive-is-zero remainder-nonneg remainder-not-positive]
                                      @add-zero-right |
                                        forall n: Int; add(n, ZERO) = n
                                        [by cite addZeroRight]
                                      @dq-plus-zero-is-dq |
                                        add(mul(d, q), ZERO) = mul(d, q)
                                        [by forall_elim(mul(d, q)) add-zero-right]
                                      @a-is-dq |
                                        a = mul(d, q)
                                        [using chain a-decomposes remainder-is-zero dq-plus-zero-is-dq]
                                      @conclusion-d-divides-a |
                                        does_divide(d, a)
                                        [using specialize dividesIntro(a, d, q) a-is-dq]
                                    }
                                  @conclusion-d-divides-a |
                                    does_divide(d, a)
                                    [by exists_elim with-remainder]
                                }
                              @d-divides-a |
                                does_divide(d, a)
                                [by exists_elim with-quotient-q]
                              // d | b: symmetric, dividing b by d.
                              @add-is-commutative |
                                forall x, y: Int; add(x, y) = add(y, x)
                                [by cite addIsCommutative]
                              @combination-commutes |
                                add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) = add(mul(b, s), mul(a, r))
                                [by forall_elim(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) add-is-commutative]
                              @d-equals-combination-swapped |
                                d = add(mul(b, s), mul(a, r))
                                [by rewrite combination-commutes d-equals-combination]
                              @b-divided-by-d |
                                exists q: Int; exists rem: Int; b = add(mul(d, q), rem) and is_nonneg(rem) and less_than(rem, d)
                                [using specialize divisionAlgorithmExists(b, d) d-is-positive]
                              @with-quotient-q2 |
                                unpack q2: Int from b-divided-by-d {
                                  @b-remainder-exists |
                                    exists rem: Int; b = add(mul(d, q2), rem) and is_nonneg(rem) and less_than(rem, d)
                                    [by hypothesis with-quotient-q2]
                                  @with-remainder-of-b |
                                    unpack rem2: Int from b-remainder-exists {
                                      @b-decomposes-and-remainder-bounded |
                                        b = add(mul(d, q2), rem2) and is_nonneg(rem2) and less_than(rem2, d)
                                        [by hypothesis with-remainder-of-b]
                                      @b-decomposes |
                                        b = add(mul(d, q2), rem2)
                                        [using tautology b-decomposes-and-remainder-bounded]
                                      @remainder-nonneg |
                                        is_nonneg(rem2)
                                        [using tautology b-decomposes-and-remainder-bounded]
                                      @remainder-less_than-d |
                                        less_than(rem2, d)
                                        [using tautology b-decomposes-and-remainder-bounded]
                                      @d-not-less_than-itself |
                                        not less_than(d, d)
                                        [using specialize lessThanIrreflexive(d)]
                                      @suppose-remainder-positive |
                                        assume less_than(ZERO, rem2) {
                                          @remainder-positive |
                                            less_than(ZERO, rem2)
                                            [by hypothesis suppose-remainder-positive]
                                          @remainder-is-member-identity |
                                            b = add(mul(d, q2), rem2) -> d = add(mul(b, s), mul(a, r)) -> rem2 = add(mul(b, sub(ONE, mul(s, q2))), mul(a, neg(mul(r, q2))))
                                            [using specialize remainderIsMember(b, a, d, s, r, q2, rem2)]
                                          @remainder-is-member-form-swapped |
                                            rem2 = add(mul(b, sub(ONE, mul(s, q2))), mul(a, neg(mul(r, q2))))
                                            [using tautology remainder-is-member-identity b-decomposes d-equals-combination-swapped]
                                          @member-form-commutes |
                                            add(mul(b, sub(ONE, mul(s, q2))), mul(a, neg(mul(r, q2)))) = add(mul(a, neg(mul(r, q2))), mul(b, sub(ONE, mul(s, q2))))
                                            [by forall_elim(mul(b, sub(ONE, mul(s, q2))), mul(a, neg(mul(r, q2)))) add-is-commutative]
                                          @remainder-is-member-form |
                                            rem2 = add(mul(a, neg(mul(r, q2))), mul(b, sub(ONE, mul(s, q2))))
                                            [by rewrite member-form-commutes remainder-is-member-form-swapped]
                                          @remainder-is-some-combination-in-nn |
                                            exists nn: Int; rem2 = add(mul(a, neg(mul(r, q2))), mul(b, nn))
                                            [by exists_intro(sub(ONE, mul(s, q2))) remainder-is-member-form]
                                          @remainder-is-a-combination |
                                            exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; rem2 = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn))
                                            [by exists_intro(neg(mul(r, q2))) remainder-is-some-combination-in-nn]
                                          @remainder-is-positive-combination |
                                            less_than(ZERO, rem2) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; rem2 = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))
                                            [by and_intro remainder-positive remainder-is-a-combination]
                                          @remainder-least-comparison |
                                            is_nonneg(rem2) -> (less_than(ZERO, rem2) and (exists mm: Int; exists nn: Int; rem2 = add(mul(a, mm), mul(b, nn)))) -> at_most(d, rem2)
                                            [by forall_elim(rem2) d-is-least]
                                          @d-at-most-remainder |
                                            at_most(d, rem2)
                                            [using tautology remainder-least-comparison remainder-nonneg remainder-is-positive-combination]
                                          @less-or-equal-split-at-d-remainder |
                                            at_most(d, rem2) -> (less_than(d, rem2) or d = rem2)
                                            [using specialize lessOrEqualSplit(d, rem2)]
                                          @d-less_than-or-equal-remainder |
                                            less_than(d, rem2) or d = rem2
                                            [by modus_ponens less-or-equal-split-at-d-remainder d-at-most-remainder]
                                          @remainder-would-be-a-smaller-member |
                                            less_than(d, d)
                                            case d-less_than-or-equal-remainder {
                                              @when-d-less_than-remainder |
                                                assume less_than(d, rem2) {
                                                  @d-less_than-remainder |
                                                    less_than(d, rem2)
                                                    [by hypothesis when-d-less_than-remainder]
                                                  @transitive-at-d-remainder-d |
                                                    less_than(d, rem2) -> less_than(rem2, d) -> less_than(d, d)
                                                    [using specialize lessThanTransitive(d, rem2, d)]
                                                  @conclusion-d-less_than-itself |
                                                    less_than(d, d)
                                                    [using tautology transitive-at-d-remainder-d d-less_than-remainder remainder-less_than-d]
                                                }
                                              @when-d-equals-remainder |
                                                assume d = rem2 {
                                                  @d-equals-remainder |
                                                    d = rem2
                                                    [by hypothesis when-d-equals-remainder]
                                                  @remainder-equals-d |
                                                    rem2 = d
                                                    [using arithmetic d-equals-remainder]
                                                  @conclusion-d-less_than-itself |
                                                    less_than(d, d)
                                                    [by rewrite remainder-equals-d remainder-less_than-d]
                                                }
                                            }
                                        }
                                      @remainder-not-positive |
                                        not less_than(ZERO, rem2)
                                        [by not_intro suppose-remainder-positive remainder-would-be-a-smaller-member d-not-less_than-itself]
                                      @nonneg-not-positive-is-zero |
                                        is_nonneg(rem2) -> (not less_than(ZERO, rem2)) -> rem2 = ZERO
                                        [using specialize nonnegNotPositiveIsZero(rem2)]
                                      @remainder-is-zero |
                                        rem2 = ZERO
                                        [using tautology nonneg-not-positive-is-zero remainder-nonneg remainder-not-positive]
                                      @add-zero-right |
                                        forall n: Int; add(n, ZERO) = n
                                        [by cite addZeroRight]
                                      @dq-plus-zero-is-dq |
                                        add(mul(d, q2), ZERO) = mul(d, q2)
                                        [by forall_elim(mul(d, q2)) add-zero-right]
                                      @b-is-dq |
                                        b = mul(d, q2)
                                        [using chain b-decomposes remainder-is-zero dq-plus-zero-is-dq]
                                      @conclusion-d-divides-b |
                                        does_divide(d, b)
                                        [using specialize dividesIntro(b, d, q2) b-is-dq]
                                    }
                                  @conclusion-d-divides-b |
                                    does_divide(d, b)
                                    [by exists_elim with-remainder-of-b]
                                }
                              @d-divides-b |
                                does_divide(d, b)
                                [by exists_elim with-quotient-q2]
                              // any common divisor e divides d = ar + bs.
                              @generalize-e |
                                fix e: Int {
                                  @given-e-divides-a |
                                    assume does_divide(e, a) {
                                      @given-e-divides-b |
                                        assume does_divide(e, b) {
                                          @e-divides-a |
                                            does_divide(e, a)
                                            [by hypothesis given-e-divides-a]
                                          @e-divides-b |
                                            does_divide(e, b)
                                            [by hypothesis given-e-divides-b]
                                          @e-divides-ar |
                                            does_divide(e, mul(a, r))
                                            [using specialize dividesMul(e, a, r) e-divides-a]
                                          @e-divides-bs |
                                            does_divide(e, mul(b, s))
                                            [using specialize dividesMul(e, b, s) e-divides-b]
                                          @divides-add |
                                            forall dd, x, y: Int; does_divide(dd, x) -> does_divide(dd, y) -> does_divide(dd, add(x, y))
                                            [by cite dividesAdd]
                                          @e-divides-sum-if-parts |
                                            does_divide(e, mul(a, r)) -> does_divide(e, mul(b, s)) -> does_divide(e, add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)))
                                            [by forall_elim(e, mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) divides-add]
                                          @e-divides-combination |
                                            does_divide(e, add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)))
                                            [using tautology e-divides-sum-if-parts e-divides-ar e-divides-bs]
                                          @conclusion-e-divides-d |
                                            does_divide(e, d)
                                            [by rewrite d-equals-combination e-divides-combination]
                                        }
                                      @e-divides-b-implies-e-divides-d |
                                        does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d)
                                        [by implies_intro given-e-divides-b]
                                    }
                                  @e-divides-a-implies-rest |
                                    does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d)
                                    [by implies_intro given-e-divides-a]
                                }
                              @common-divisor-divides-d |
                                forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d)
                                [by forall_intro generalize-e]
                              // assemble the five defining properties of the gcd.
                              @d-is-some-combination-in-s |
                                exists s2: Int; d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s2))
                                [by exists_intro(s) d-equals-combination]
                              @d-is-a-combination-again |
                                exists r2: Int; exists s2: Int; d = add(mul(a, r2), mul(b, s2))
                                [by exists_intro(r) d-is-some-combination-in-s]
                              @d-positive-and-divides-a |
                                less_than(ZERO, d) and does_divide(d, a)
                                [by and_intro d-is-positive d-divides-a]
                              @d-divides-a-and-b |
                                less_than(ZERO, d) and does_divide(d, a) and does_divide(d, b)
                                [by and_intro d-positive-and-divides-a d-divides-b]
                              @d-divides-and-greatest |
                                less_than(ZERO, d) and does_divide(d, a) and does_divide(d, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d))
                                [by and_intro d-divides-a-and-b common-divisor-divides-d]
                              @d-has-all-gcd-properties |
                                less_than(ZERO, d) and does_divide(d, a) and does_divide(d, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d)) and (exists r2: Int; exists s2: Int; d = add(mul(a, r2), mul(b, s2)))
                                [by and_intro d-divides-and-greatest d-is-a-combination-again]
                              @conclusion-bezout |
                                exists d2: Int; less_than(ZERO, d2) and does_divide(d2, a) and does_divide(d2, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d2)) and (exists r2: Int; exists s2: Int; d2 = add(mul(a, r2), mul(b, s2)))
                                [by exists_intro(d) d-has-all-gcd-properties]
                            }
                          @conclusion-bezout |
                            exists d2: Int; less_than(ZERO, d2) and does_divide(d2, a) and does_divide(d2, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d2)) and (exists r2: Int; exists s2: Int; d2 = add(mul(a, r2), mul(b, s2)))
                            [by exists_elim with-coefficient-s]
                        }
                      @conclusion-bezout |
                        exists d2: Int; less_than(ZERO, d2) and does_divide(d2, a) and does_divide(d2, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d2)) and (exists r2: Int; exists s2: Int; d2 = add(mul(a, r2), mul(b, s2)))
                        [by exists_elim with-coefficient-r]
                    }
                  @conclusion-bezout |
                    exists d2: Int; less_than(ZERO, d2) and does_divide(d2, a) and does_divide(d2, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d2)) and (exists r2: Int; exists s2: Int; d2 = add(mul(a, r2), mul(b, s2)))
                    [by exists_elim with-least-member-d]
                }
              @b-nonzero-gives-bezout |
                (b != ZERO)
                  -> exists d: Int; less_than(ZERO, d) and does_divide(d, a) and does_divide(d, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d)) and (exists r: Int; exists s: Int; d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)))
                [by implies_intro given-b-nonzero]
            }
          @a-nonzero-gives-bezout |
            (a != ZERO)
              -> (b != ZERO) -> exists d: Int; less_than(ZERO, d) and does_divide(d, a) and does_divide(d, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d)) and (exists r: Int; exists s: Int; d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)))
            [by implies_intro given-a-nonzero]
        }
      @discharge-b |
        forall b: Int; (a != ZERO) -> (b != ZERO) -> exists d: Int; less_than(ZERO, d) and does_divide(d, a) and does_divide(d, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d)) and (exists r: Int; exists s: Int; d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)))
        [by forall_intro generalize-b]
    }
  @conclusion |
    forall a, b: Int; (a != ZERO) -> (b != ZERO) -> exists d: Int; less_than(ZERO, d) and does_divide(d, a) and does_divide(d, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d)) and (exists r: Int; exists s: Int; d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)))
    [by forall_intro generalize-a]
qed
```

## The Coprime Corollary

> **Corollary 2.11.** Let $a$ and $b$ be two integers that are relatively prime.
> Then there exist integers $r$ and $s$ such that $ar + bs = 1$.

If `a` and `b` are coprime — every common divisor is `±1` — Bézout's positive
common divisor `d = gcd(a,b)` must itself be `1` or `−1`. It is positive
(`less_than(ZERO, d)`), and `−1` is *not* positive (`negOneLessThanZero` puts `−1 < 0`,
which together with `0 < −1` and transitivity would give `0 < 0`), so `d = 1` and
Bézout's identity `d = ar + bs` becomes `ar + bs = 1`.

```bpa
theorem coprimeBezout: forall a, b: Int; a != ZERO -> b != ZERO ->
  (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> (e = ONE or e = neg(ONE)))
  -> exists r: Int; exists s: Int; add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) = ONE
proof
  @generalize-a |
    fix a: Int {
      @generalize-b |
        fix b: Int {
          @given-a-nonzero |
            assume a != ZERO {
              @given-b-nonzero |
                assume b != ZERO {
                  @given-coprime |
                    assume forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> (e = ONE or e = neg(ONE)) {
                      @a-nonzero |
                        a != ZERO
                        [by hypothesis given-a-nonzero]
                      @b-nonzero |
                        b != ZERO
                        [by hypothesis given-b-nonzero]
                      @coprime |
                        forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> (e = ONE or e = neg(ONE))
                        [by hypothesis given-coprime]
                      // Bézout supplies a positive gcd d = ar + bs dividing a and b.
                      @gcd-exists |
                        exists d: Int; less_than(ZERO, d) and does_divide(d, a) and does_divide(d, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d)) and (exists r: Int; exists s: Int; d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)))
                        [using specialize bezout(a, b) a-nonzero b-nonzero]
                      @with-gcd-d |
                        unpack d: Int from gcd-exists {
                          @d-has-all-gcd-properties |
                            less_than(ZERO, d) and does_divide(d, a) and does_divide(d, b) and (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> does_divide(e, d)) and (exists r: Int; exists s: Int; d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)))
                            [by hypothesis with-gcd-d]
                          @d-is-positive |
                            less_than(ZERO, d)
                            [using tautology d-has-all-gcd-properties]
                          @d-divides-a |
                            does_divide(d, a)
                            [using tautology d-has-all-gcd-properties]
                          @d-divides-b |
                            does_divide(d, b)
                            [using tautology d-has-all-gcd-properties]
                          @d-is-a-combination |
                            exists r: Int; exists s: Int; d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s))
                            [using tautology d-has-all-gcd-properties]
                          // coprimality forces the gcd to be 1 or -1.
                          @coprime-at-d |
                            does_divide(d, a) -> does_divide(d, b) -> (d = ONE or d = neg(ONE))
                            [by forall_elim(d) coprime]
                          @d-is-one-or-neg-one |
                            d = ONE or d = neg(ONE)
                            [using tautology coprime-at-d d-divides-a d-divides-b]
                          @conclusion-bezout-identity |
                            exists r: Int; exists s: Int; add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) = ONE
                            case d-is-one-or-neg-one {
                              @when-d-is-one |
                                assume d = ONE {
                                  @d-is-one |
                                    d = ONE
                                    [by hypothesis when-d-is-one]
                                  @with-coefficient-r |
                                    unpack r: Int from d-is-a-combination {
                                      @d-is-combination-in-r |
                                        exists s: Int; d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s))
                                        [by hypothesis with-coefficient-r]
                                      @with-coefficient-s |
                                        unpack s: Int from d-is-combination-in-r {
                                          @d-equals-combination |
                                            d = add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s))
                                            [by hypothesis with-coefficient-s]
                                          @combination-equals-one |
                                            add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) = ONE
                                            [using chain d-equals-combination d-is-one]
                                          @combination-in-s-is-one |
                                            exists s2: Int; add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s2)) = ONE
                                            [by exists_intro(s) combination-equals-one]
                                          @conclusion-bezout-identity |
                                            exists r2: Int; exists s2: Int; add(mul(a, r2), mul(b, s2)) = ONE
                                            [by exists_intro(r) combination-in-s-is-one]
                                        }
                                      @conclusion-bezout-identity |
                                        exists r2: Int; exists s2: Int; add(mul(a, r2), mul(b, s2)) = ONE
                                        [by exists_elim with-coefficient-s]
                                    }
                                  @conclusion-bezout-identity |
                                    exists r2: Int; exists s2: Int; add(mul(a, r2), mul(b, s2)) = ONE
                                    [by exists_elim with-coefficient-r]
                                }
                              @when-d-is-neg-one |
                                assume d = neg(ONE) {
                                  @d-is-neg-one |
                                    d = neg(ONE)
                                    [by hypothesis when-d-is-neg-one]
                                  // d positive but d = -1 < 0 is impossible.
                                  @neg-one-is-positive |
                                    less_than(ZERO, neg(ONE))
                                    [by rewrite d-is-neg-one d-is-positive]
                                  @neg-one-is-negative |
                                    less_than(neg(ONE), ZERO)
                                    [by cite negOneLessThanZero]
                                  @less-than-transitive |
                                    forall x, y, z: Int; less_than(x, y) -> less_than(y, z) -> less_than(x, z)
                                    [by cite lessThanTransitive]
                                  @transitive-through-neg-one |
                                    less_than(ZERO, neg(ONE)) -> less_than(neg(ONE), ZERO) -> less_than(ZERO, ZERO)
                                    [by forall_elim(ZERO, neg(ONE), ZERO) less-than-transitive]
                                  @zero-less_than-itself |
                                    less_than(ZERO, ZERO)
                                    [using tautology transitive-through-neg-one neg-one-is-positive neg-one-is-negative]
                                  @zero-not-less_than-itself |
                                    not less_than(ZERO, ZERO)
                                    [using specialize lessThanIrreflexive(ZERO)]
                                  @conclusion-bezout-identity |
                                    exists r2: Int; exists s2: Int; add(mul(a, r2), mul(b, s2)) = ONE
                                    [by absurd zero-less_than-itself zero-not-less_than-itself]
                                }
                            }
                        }
                      @conclusion-bezout-identity |
                        exists r2: Int; exists s2: Int; add(mul(a, r2), mul(b, s2)) = ONE
                        [by exists_elim with-gcd-d]
                    }
                  @coprime-implies-bezout-identity |
                    (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> (e = ONE or e = neg(ONE)))
                      -> exists r: Int; exists s: Int; add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) = ONE
                    [by implies_intro given-coprime]
                }
              @b-nonzero-implies-coprime-bezout |
                (b != ZERO)
                  -> (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> (e = ONE or e = neg(ONE)))
                  -> exists r: Int; exists s: Int; add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) = ONE
                [by implies_intro given-b-nonzero]
            }
          @a-nonzero-implies-coprime-bezout |
            (a != ZERO)
              -> (b != ZERO)
              -> (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> (e = ONE or e = neg(ONE)))
              -> exists r: Int; exists s: Int; add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) = ONE
            [by implies_intro given-a-nonzero]
        }
      @discharge-b |
        forall b: Int; (a != ZERO) -> (b != ZERO) -> (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> (e = ONE or e = neg(ONE))) -> exists r: Int; exists s: Int; add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) = ONE
        [by forall_intro generalize-b]
    }
  @conclusion |
    forall a, b: Int; (a != ZERO) -> (b != ZERO) -> (forall e: Int; does_divide(e, a) -> does_divide(e, b) -> (e = ONE or e = neg(ONE))) -> exists r: Int; exists s: Int; add(mul(a, r), mul(b, s)) = ONE
    [by forall_intro generalize-a]
qed
```

## Prime Numbers

A **prime** is an integer `p > 1` whose only *positive* divisors are `1` and `p`
itself (Judson, Definition 2.10). Since `bpa` predicates are opaque, we spell the
condition out inline as the conjunction

```
less_than(ONE, p) and (forall d: Int; less_than(ZERO, d) -> does_divide(d, p) -> (d = ONE or d = p))
```

wherever "`p` is prime" is needed. This section proves the two theorems that make
primes the multiplicative atoms of `ℤ`: **Euclid's Lemma** — a prime dividing a
product divides one of the factors — and, as the classical corollary, a note on
the **infinitude of primes**. Euclid's Lemma rests on `coprimeBezout` (proved
above) and on the ℤ number-theory library; we bring in the last few names the
argument spells out, plus the finite-product sequence library for the
infinitude discussion.

```bpa
import sequence <<< "std/integer-sequence.bpa"

axiom mulZeroLeft = ring.mulZeroLeft
axiom mulSuccLeft = ring.mulSuccLeft
axiom negZero = int_divides.negZero
theorem mulIsCommutative = order.mulIsCommutative
theorem mulNegLeft = order.mulNegLeft
theorem nonnegNotLessThanZero = wellordering.nonnegNotLessThanZero
theorem dividesRefl = int_divides.dividesRefl

sort Seq = sequence.Seq
func at = sequence.at
func productUpTo = sequence.productUpTo
pred inSeqBefore = sequence.inSeqBefore
axiom productUpToZero = sequence.productUpToZero
axiom productUpToSucc = sequence.productUpToSucc
theorem everyEntryDividesProduct = sequence.everyEntryDividesProduct
theorem memberDividesProduct = sequence.memberDividesProduct
theorem primeFactorExists = sequence.primeFactorExists
theorem dividesSubtracts = sequence.dividesSubtracts
theorem oneAtMostPositive = sequence.oneAtMostPositive
theorem divisorAtMost = sequence.divisorAtMost
theorem lastEntryDividesProduct = sequence.lastEntryDividesProduct
theorem atMostThenLessThan = int_divides.atMostThenLessThan
theorem mulCancelLeft = int_divides.mulCancelLeft
theorem mulCancelRight = int_divides.mulCancelRight
theorem lessThanSelf = wellordering.lessThanSelf

// -- sequence-construction axioms + the order/arith facts the Fundamental
//    Theorem of Arithmetic (less_than) needs but the sections above did not ------
axiom seqSingletonExists = sequence.seqSingletonExists
axiom seqConcatExists = sequence.seqConcatExists
axiom seqRemoveExists = sequence.seqRemoveExists
axiom seqPrependExists = sequence.seqPrependExists
axiom seqShiftPastExists = sequence.seqShiftPastExists
theorem nonnegStrongInduction = int_divides.nonnegStrongInduction
axiom nonnegInduction = order.nonnegInduction
theorem productWithPositiveIsPositive = int_divides.productWithPositiveIsPositive
theorem additionPreservesOrder = order.additionPreservesOrder
theorem lessThanAddCancelLeft = order.lessThanAddCancelLeft
theorem lessThanSuccSplit = wellordering.lessThanSuccSplit
theorem signTrichotomy = order.signTrichotomy
theorem nonnegMul = order.nonnegMul
theorem nonnegAdd = order.nonnegAdd
theorem lessOrEqualRefl = order.lessOrEqualRefl
theorem lessOrEqualAntisymmetric = order.lessOrEqualAntisymmetric
// `at_most` (= less_or_equal) is now `less_than or =`, so its old gap-existential
// intro/elim AXIOMS are gone. `lessOrEqualIsGapExistential` proves at_most(a,b) iff
// (exists d; is_nonneg(d) and add(a,d)=b) — recover intro/elim as local theorems by
// projecting each direction of that iff.
theorem lessOrEqualIsGapExistential = order.lessOrEqualIsGapExistential
```

```bpa
theorem lessOrEqualElim: forall a, b: Int; at_most(a, b) -> exists d: Int; is_nonneg(d) and add(a, d) = b
proof
  @generalize-a |
    fix a: Int {
      @generalize-b |
        fix b: Int {
          @gap-iff-at-a-b |
            at_most(a, b) iff (exists d: Int; is_nonneg(d) and add(a, d) = b)
            [using specialize lessOrEqualIsGapExistential(a, b)]
          @elim-at-a-b |
            at_most(a, b) -> exists d: Int; is_nonneg(d) and add(a, d) = b
            [by iff_elim_forward gap-iff-at-a-b]
        }
      @discharge-b |
        forall b: Int; at_most(a, b) -> exists d: Int; is_nonneg(d) and add(a, d) = b
        [by forall_intro generalize-b]
    }
  @conclusion |
    forall a, b: Int; at_most(a, b) -> exists d: Int; is_nonneg(d) and add(a, d) = b
    [by forall_intro generalize-a]
qed

theorem lessOrEqualIntro: forall a, g, b: Int; is_nonneg(g) -> add(a, g) = b -> at_most(a, b)
proof
  @generalize-a |
    fix a: Int {
      @generalize-g |
        fix g: Int {
          @generalize-b |
            fix b: Int {
              @given-nonneg-g |
                assume is_nonneg(g) {
                  @given-gap |
                    assume add(a, g) = b {
                      @nonneg-g |
                        is_nonneg(g)
                        [by hypothesis given-nonneg-g]
                      @the-gap |
                        add(a, g) = b
                        [by hypothesis given-gap]
                      @nonneg-and-gap |
                        is_nonneg(g) and add(a, g) = b
                        [by and_intro nonneg-g the-gap]
                      @gap-exists |
                        exists d: Int; is_nonneg(d) and add(a, d) = b
                        [by exists_intro(g) nonneg-and-gap]
                      @gap-iff-at-a-b |
                        at_most(a, b) iff (exists d: Int; is_nonneg(d) and add(a, d) = b)
                        [using specialize lessOrEqualIsGapExistential(a, b)]
                      @gap-gives-at-most |
                        (exists d: Int; is_nonneg(d) and add(a, d) = b) -> at_most(a, b)
                        [by iff_elim_backward gap-iff-at-a-b]
                      @a-at-most-b |
                        at_most(a, b)
                        [by modus_ponens gap-gives-at-most gap-exists]
                    }
                  @gap-gives-le |
                    add(a, g) = b -> at_most(a, b)
                    [by implies_intro given-gap]
                }
              @nonneg-gives |
                is_nonneg(g) -> add(a, g) = b -> at_most(a, b)
                [by implies_intro given-nonneg-g]
            }
          @discharge-b |
            forall b: Int; is_nonneg(g) -> add(a, g) = b -> at_most(a, b)
            [by forall_intro generalize-b]
        }
      @discharge-g |
        forall g, b: Int; is_nonneg(g) -> add(a, g) = b -> at_most(a, b)
        [by forall_intro generalize-g]
    }
  @conclusion |
    forall a, g, b: Int; is_nonneg(g) -> add(a, g) = b -> at_most(a, b)
    [by forall_intro generalize-a]
qed
```

The elided ring algebra Euclid's Lemma needs — collapsing `b·(ar+ps)` to a
sum of two `p`-related products — is a nonlinear identity handled by the
`polynomial` tactic. That tactic expects the ring's associativity/commutativity
lemmas *and their left-swap forms* in scope; ℤ has the swaps only implicitly, so
we prove the three names it looks up (`mulOneLeft`, `mulLeftSwap`, `addLeftSwap`)
first. `mul(1, n) = n` mirrors `mulOneRight` on the left, unfolding `1 = succ 0`
through `mulSuccLeft`.

```bpa
theorem mulOneLeft: forall n: Int; mul(ONE, n) = n
proof
  @generalize-n |
    fix n: Int {
      @one-is-succ-zero |
        ONE = succ(ZERO)
        [by cite oneIsSuccZero]
      @mul-succ-zero-n |
        mul(succ(ZERO), n) = add(mul(ZERO, n), n)
        [using specialize mulSuccLeft(ZERO, n)]
      @mul-zero-n-is-zero |
        mul(ZERO, n) = ZERO
        [using specialize mulZeroLeft(n)]
      @zero-plus-n-is-n |
        add(ZERO, n) = n
        [using specialize addZeroLeft(n)]
      // unfold ONE, collapse mul(ZERO, n) and add(ZERO, n).
      @conclusion-mul-one-left |
        mul(ONE, n) = n
        [using arithmetic one-is-succ-zero mul-succ-zero-n mul-zero-n-is-zero zero-plus-n-is-n]
    }
  @conclusion |
    forall n: Int; mul(ONE, n) = n
    [by forall_intro generalize-n]
qed
```

The left-swap forms `a·(b·c) = b·(a·c)` and `a+(b+c) = b+(a+c)` reassociate,
commute the exposed pair, then reassociate back.

```bpa
theorem mulLeftSwap: forall a, b, c: Int; mul(a, mul(b, c)) = mul(b, mul(a, c))
proof
  @generalize-a |
    fix a: Int {
      @generalize-b |
        fix b: Int {
          @generalize-c |
            fix c: Int {
              @abc-regroups-flat |
                mul(mul(a, b), c) = mul(a, mul(b, c))
                [using specialize mulIsAssociative(a, b, c)]
              @a-b-swaps |
                mul(a, b) = mul(b, a)
                [using specialize mulIsCommutative(a, b)]
              @ba-c-regroups |
                mul(mul(b, a), c) = mul(b, mul(a, c))
                [using specialize mulIsAssociative(b, a, c)]
              @conclusion-mul-left-swap |
                mul(a, mul(b, c)) = mul(b, mul(a, c))
                [using chain abc-regroups-flat a-b-swaps ba-c-regroups]
            }
          @discharge-c |
            forall c: Int; mul(a, mul(b, c)) = mul(b, mul(a, c))
            [by forall_intro generalize-c]
        }
      @discharge-b |
        forall b, c: Int; mul(a, mul(b, c)) = mul(b, mul(a, c))
        [by forall_intro generalize-b]
    }
  @conclusion |
    forall a, b, c: Int; mul(a, mul(b, c)) = mul(b, mul(a, c))
    [by forall_intro generalize-a]
qed
```

The additive left-swap is the identical argument on `add` — proved
(`addLeftSwap`) just before `remainderIsMember` above, where the `arithmetic`
certifier first needs it. (TEMPORARY placement: it belongs here in Judson's
build order; the model-spaces refactor, which frees the certifier from needing
its rule lemmas pre-declared, will restore it to this spot.)

A small positivity fact used repeatedly: `0 < 1`, the gap being `0`, since
`add(0, succ 0) = succ 0 = 1`.

```bpa
theorem zeroLessThanOne = order.zeroLessThanOne
```

`neg(e)` divides whatever `e` divides: unfolding `n = e·k` gives
`n = neg(e)·neg(k)` (`mulNegLeft`, `mulNegRight`, `negNeg` cancel the two sign
flips), so `neg(e) | n`. This lets the coprimality argument reduce a negative
common divisor to its positive counterpart.

```bpa
theorem negDivides: forall e, n: Int; does_divide(e, n) -> does_divide(neg(e), n)
proof
  @generalize-e |
    fix e: Int {
      @generalize-n |
        fix n: Int {
          @given-e-divides-n |
            assume does_divide(e, n) {
              @e-divides-n |
                does_divide(e, n)
                [by hypothesis given-e-divides-n]
              @quotient-exists |
                exists k: Int; n = mul(e, k)
                [using specialize dividesElim(e, n) e-divides-n]
              @with-quotient-k |
                unpack k: Int from quotient-exists {
                  @n-is-e-times-k |
                    n = mul(e, k)
                    [by hypothesis with-quotient-k]
                  @e-times-k-is-neg-e-times-neg-k |
                    mul(e, k) = mul(neg(e), neg(k))
                    [using simplify mulNegLeft mulNegRight negNeg]
                  @n-is-neg-e-times-neg-k |
                    n = mul(neg(e), neg(k))
                    [by rewrite e-times-k-is-neg-e-times-neg-k n-is-e-times-k]
                  @divides-intro |
                    forall m, d, j: Int; m = mul(d, j) -> does_divide(d, m)
                    [by cite dividesIntro]
                  @neg-e-divides-n-if-product |
                    n = mul(neg(e), neg(k)) -> does_divide(neg(e), n)
                    [by forall_elim(n, neg(e), neg(k)) divides-intro]
                  @neg-e-divides-n |
                    does_divide(neg(e), n)
                    [by modus_ponens neg-e-divides-n-if-product n-is-neg-e-times-neg-k]
                }
              @neg-e-divides-n |
                does_divide(neg(e), n)
```
